P0: DEMOGRAPHIC DETAILS.
P1: a.) Name (with an asterisk for compulsory).
P2: b.) Ethnicity (with an asterisk for compulsory).
P3: All the validated ethnicities to be entered.
P4: Like Asian, African, etc.
P5: c.) Age (with an asterisk for compulsory).
P6: Age to be typed with a scroll up increase & scroll down decrease button & there must be years as the unit, no necessary for months & years.
P7: Next block must be where the date of birth will be typed in the format of DD/MM/YYYY & based on that the age will be directly calculated in the format of Years, Months & Days in the block next.
P8: So when it would be typed, it must be directly typed in numbers & the software must understand it in DD/MM/YYYY.
And there must also be a provision for selection of date of birth from the chart, where the months, days & multiple years are present.
P9: But if date of birth is selected then age type must be disabled.
P10: d.) Sex (with an asterisk for compulsory).
P11: i.) Male.
P12: ii.) Female.
P13: iii.) Transgender.
P14: iv.) Prefer not to say.
P15: e.) Height (with an asterisk for compulsory).
P16: i.) With unit of centimeters.
P17: ii.) It'll calculated in to foots & inches which will be the next block.
P18: iii.) If typed in foots & inches then it'll be calculated in centimeters.
P19: iv.) But when any is selected other will be filled automatically & manual typing will be disabled.
P20: Height to be typed with a scroll up increase & scroll down decrease button.
P21: f.) Weight (with an asterisk for compulsory).
P22: i.) With unit of kilograms.
P23: ii.) It'll be directly calculated in pounds which will be the next block.
P24: iii.) If typed in pounds then it'll be directly calculated to kilograms.
P25: iv.) But when any is selected the manual typing will be disabled.
P26: Weight to be typed with a scroll up increase & scroll down decrease button.
P27: g.) BMI (with an asterisk for compulsory).
P28: Here it'll be a unit of Kg/m2.
P29: There shall be no option of manual typing & it must be calculated directly.
P30: h.) Based on the validated scale decide them in to underweight, normal, overweight, grades of overweight, I, II, III, & obese.
P31: Now this calculation must be on the basis of World Health Organisation guidelines.
P32: i.) Physical Activity.
P33: i.) Sedentary.
P34: ii.) Moderate.
P35: iii.) Heavy.
P36: iv.) Can't say.
P38: For physical activity, a useful adult reference is 150–300 minutes of moderate-intensity aerobic activity per week, or 75–150 minutes of vigorous activity, or an equivalent combination. Children and adolescents aged 5–17 have a different recommendation: an average of at least 60 minutes of moderate-to-vigorous activity daily.
P39: And it must work other way round as well, & in case they do this or that, the other must be physically disabled for entering.
P40: If can't say then select the duration of activity & based on it grade as per sedentary, moderate or heavy in the next column as per guidelines.

P686: ONCOGUIDE — Common Symptom and Red-Flag Questionnaire
P687: Module ID: COMMON-RED-FLAGS-V1
Purpose: Identify symptoms requiring clinical assessment and activate relevant cancer-specific pathways without duplicating questions.
P688: C001 — Unintentional Weight Loss
P689: Question: Have you lost weight without trying, for example, without changing your diet or physical activity?
P690: Options:
P691: No
P692: Yes, a small amount
P693: Yes, a noticeable amount
P694: Yes, a substantial or continuing amount
P695: Not sure
P696: If Yes:
P697: Approximately how much weight have you lost? ___ kg
P698: Over what period? Less than 1 month / 1–3 months / 4–6 months / More than 6 months / Not sure
P699: What was your approximate weight before the loss? ___ kg / Not sure
P700: Is the weight loss continuing? Yes / No / Not sure
P701: Routing: Consider the relevant pathway in combination with other symptoms, examination findings and risk factors. Weight loss alone does not identify a particular cancer.
P702: C002 — Unexplained Fever
P703: Question: Have you had a fever without an obvious explanation, or a fever that keeps returning?
P704: Options:
P705: No
P706: Yes, for a short period
P707: Yes, it keeps returning
P708: Yes, it has persisted
P709: Not sure
P710: If Yes:
P711: How long has this been happening? Less than 1 week / 1–2 weeks / More than 2 weeks / Recurrent over a longer period / Not sure
P712: Is there a known or suspected infection or another explanation? Yes / No / Not sure
P713: Routing: Persistent or recurrent unexplained fever may support hematologic or other relevant pathways when combined with additional findings. Assess acute illness independently.
P714: C003 — Repeated Night Sweats
P715: Question: Have you repeatedly experienced night sweats that soak your clothes or bedsheets, even when your room is not excessively warm?
P716: Options:
P717: No
P718: Yes, occasionally
P719: Yes, repeatedly
P720: Yes, repeatedly along with fever or unexplained weight loss
P721: Not sure
P722: If Yes: How long has this been occurring? Less than 1 week / 1–4 weeks / More than 1 month / Not sure
P723: Routing: Repeated drenching night sweats, particularly with fever, weight loss or enlarged lymph nodes, may support hematologic assessment. Occasional sweating alone should not trigger a strong cancer alert.
P724: C004 — Persistent Fatigue or Weakness
P725: Question: Have you experienced unusual tiredness or weakness that does not improve adequately with rest?
P726: Options:
P727: No
P728: Yes, occasionally
P729: Yes, persistently
P730: Yes, it is worsening or affecting daily activities
P731: Not sure
P732: If Yes:
P733: How long has this been occurring? Less than 2 weeks / 2–4 weeks / More than 4 weeks / Not sure
P734: Is it worsening? Yes / No / Not sure
P735: Routing: Fatigue is highly nonspecific. Consider it alongside other symptoms and possible non-cancer causes; do not activate a cancer pathway based on fatigue alone.
P736: C005 — New Lump or Swelling
P737: Question: Have you noticed a new or unexplained lump or swelling anywhere on your body?
P738: Options:
P739: No
P740: Yes
P741: Not sure
P742: If Yes:
P743: Where is it? Neck / Breast or chest / Armpit / Groin / Abdomen / Mouth or jaw / Other location
P744: When did you first notice it? Within the past 2 weeks / 2–6 weeks ago / More than 6 weeks ago / Not sure
P745: Has it increased in size? Yes / No / Not sure
P746: Is it painful? Yes / No / Sometimes / Not sure
P747: Routing: Activate the pathway relevant to the anatomical site and associated findings. Neck lumps, breast lumps, oral lesions and enlarged lymph nodes require different rules. Do not activate every cancer pathway for a generic lump.
P748: C006 — Unexplained Bruising or Bleeding
P749: Question: Have you experienced unexplained bruising, unusual bleeding or small red-purple spots on your skin?
P750: Options:
P751: No
P752: Yes, unexplained bruising
P753: Yes, unusual bleeding
P754: Yes, small red-purple spots
P755: Yes, more than one of these
P756: Not sure
P757: If Yes:
P758: When did it begin? Recently / Several weeks ago / Longer ago / Not sure
P759: Has it happened repeatedly? Once / Repeatedly / Increasingly / Not sure
P760: Is the bleeding heavy, ongoing or difficult to stop? Yes / No / Not applicable / Not sure
P761: Routing: Consider hematologic assessment when appropriate, but assess the actual bleeding site and other causes. Heavy or uncontrolled bleeding requires urgent medical assessment independently of cancer risk.
P762: C007 — Blood in Stools or Black Stools
P763: Question: Have you noticed blood in your stool or stools that are black and tar-like?
P764: Options:
P765: No
P766: Bright-red blood
P767: Dark-red or maroon blood
P768: Black, tar-like stools
P769: Other unusual stool appearance
P770: Not sure
P771: If Yes:
P772: Did this occur once or repeatedly? Once / Repeatedly / Not sure
P773: Was the blood mixed with the stool or mainly on the toilet paper or stool surface? Mixed / Mainly on paper or surface / Not sure / Not applicable
P774: Routing: Consider the colorectal pathway and other appropriate gastrointestinal assessment. Do not ask the same blood-in-stool question again inside the colorectal pathway.
P775: Safety: Black, tar-like stools or substantial bleeding can indicate gastrointestinal bleeding and may require urgent assessment. Do not assume the cause is cancer.
P776: C008 — Blood in Urine
P777: Question: Have you noticed visible blood in your urine, or urine that appeared pink, red or brown and may have contained blood?
P778: Options:
P779: No
P780: Yes, once
P781: Yes, repeatedly
P782: Urine appeared discoloured, but I am unsure whether it was blood
P783: Not sure
P784: If Yes:
P785: Was there pain or burning when passing urine? Yes / No / Sometimes / Not sure
P786: Did you have difficulty passing urine? Yes / No / Not sure
P787: Routing: Refer for appropriate urinary-tract assessment. Blood in urine does not automatically indicate prostate cancer; use anatomy, associated symptoms and clinical assessment to determine the appropriate pathway.
P788: Safety: Visible blood in urine warrants medical assessment even when painless.
P789: C009 — Coughing Up Blood
P790: Question: Have you coughed up blood or blood-streaked mucus?
P791: Options:
P792: No
P793: Yes, small streaks of blood
P794: Yes, more than small streaks
P795: Yes, repeatedly
P796: Not sure
P797: If Yes:
P798: When did this occur? Today / Within the past week / More than a week ago / Repeatedly over a longer period
P799: Are you currently coughing up blood? Yes / No / Not sure
P800: Are you also experiencing breathing difficulty or chest pain? Yes / No
P801: Routing: Consider lung and other relevant respiratory pathways. Determine urgency from the amount of bleeding, whether it is ongoing and associated symptoms.
P802: Safety: Significant or ongoing coughing up blood, particularly with breathing difficulty, requires urgent or emergency assessment.
P803: C010 — Difficulty or Pain When Swallowing
P804: Question: Have you developed difficulty or pain when swallowing food, liquids or saliva?
P805: Options:
P806: No
P807: Difficulty swallowing solid food
P808: Difficulty swallowing liquids
P809: Difficulty swallowing both solids and liquids
P810: Pain when swallowing
P811: Food feels stuck in the throat or chest
P812: Not sure
P813: If Yes:
P814: When did it begin? Within the past 2 weeks / 2–6 weeks ago / More than 6 weeks ago / Not sure
P815: Is it getting worse? Yes / No / Not sure
P816: Routing: Consider oesophageal, oral, hypopharyngeal or other head-and-neck pathways according to the symptom pattern and associated findings.
P817: Safety: Inability to swallow saliva or liquids, especially with choking or breathing difficulty, requires urgent assessment.
P818: C011 — Persistent Voice Change or Hoarseness
P819: Question: Have you experienced a voice change or hoarseness that is persistent or keeps returning without an obvious explanation?
P820: Options:
P821: No
P822: Yes, intermittent
P823: Yes, persistent
P824: Yes, worsening
P825: Not sure
P826: If Yes: For how long? Less than 2 weeks / 2–3 weeks / More than 3 weeks / Not sure
P827: Routing: Consider the laryngeal, thyroid or other head-and-neck pathway, particularly if there is a neck lump, swallowing difficulty or breathing symptom. Persistent unexplained hoarseness needs clinical assessment; do not rely on this question alone to determine urgency.
P828: C012 — Persistent Abdominal or Pelvic Bloating
P829: Question: Have you experienced abdominal or pelvic bloating or swelling that is persistent, recurrent or increasing?
P830: Options:
P831: No
P832: Yes, occasionally
P833: Yes, repeatedly
P834: Yes, persistently or increasingly
P835: Not sure
P836: If Yes:
P837: Where is the swelling or discomfort mainly located? Upper abdomen / Lower abdomen or pelvis / Throughout the abdomen / Not sure
P838: Do you also experience unusual fullness after eating a small amount? Yes / No / Not sure
P839: Do you also experience new or persistent urinary urgency or frequency? Yes / No / Not sure
P840: Routing: Consider ovarian, gastrointestinal or other relevant assessment according to anatomy, age, symptom persistence and associated findings. Bloating alone is not specific for ovarian cancer.
P841: C013 — Persistent Change in Bowel Habits
P842: Question: Have you developed a persistent or unexplained change in your usual bowel habits?
P843: Options:
P844: No
P845: Persistent constipation
P846: Repeated or persistent diarrhoea
P847: Alternating constipation and diarrhoea
P848: A persistent change in stool frequency, size or consistency
P849: Not sure
P850: If Yes:
P851: How long has the change lasted? Less than 2 weeks / 2–4 weeks / More than 4 weeks / Not sure
P852: Is it worsening or recurring? Yes / No / Not sure
P853: Routing: Consider the colorectal pathway and other gastrointestinal causes. Use C007 for blood in stool rather than repeating that question here.
P854: C014 — Reduced Appetite or Early Fullness
P855: Question: Have you experienced an unexplained change in appetite or feeling unusually full after eating only a small amount?
P856: Options:
P857: No
P858: Reduced appetite
P859: Feeling full unusually quickly after starting a meal
P860: Both reduced appetite and early fullness
P861: Not sure
P862: If Yes:
P863: How long has this been occurring? Less than 2 weeks / 2–4 weeks / More than 4 weeks / Not sure
P864: Is it associated with unintentional weight loss? Yes / No / Not sure
P865: Routing: Consider relevant gastrointestinal, liver, ovarian or other pathways only in combination with the broader clinical picture. Avoid activating several pathways solely because of reduced appetite.
P866: C015 — Persistent or Unexplained Pain
P867: Question: Have you experienced pain that is persistent, recurrent, worsening or unexplained?
P868: Options:
P869: No
P870: Yes, persistent pain
P871: Yes, recurrent pain
P872: Yes, worsening pain
P873: Not sure
P874: If Yes:
P875: Where is the pain? Head or face / Mouth or throat / Chest / Upper abdomen / Lower abdomen or pelvis / Back or bones / Other location
P876: How long has it been present? Less than 2 weeks / 2–6 weeks / More than 6 weeks / Not sure
P877: Is it worsening? Yes / No / Not sure
P878: Does it interfere with normal activities or sleep? Yes / No / Sometimes
P879: Routing: Use the pain location and associated findings to guide the relevant pathway. Pain alone is not cancer-specific, and severe acute pain must be assessed independently.
P880: C016 — Breathing Difficulty
P881: Question: Have you experienced unexplained difficulty breathing or shortness of breath?
P882: Options:
P883: No
P884: Yes, only during physical activity
P885: Yes, with mild activity or activities that were previously easy
P886: Yes, while resting
P887: Yes, sudden or severe difficulty breathing
P888: Not sure
P889: If Yes:
P890: When did it begin? Suddenly / Gradually / Not sure
P891: Is it worsening? Yes / No / Not sure
P892: Is there noisy breathing at rest? Yes / No / Not sure
P893: Is there associated chest pain or coughing up blood? Yes / No
P894: Routing: Consider respiratory and other appropriate clinical assessment. Do not assume that breathing difficulty is caused by cancer.
P895: Safety: Sudden or severe breathing difficulty, noisy breathing at rest, or breathing difficulty with significant chest pain requires emergency assessment. Do not delay care to complete the questionnaire.
P897: Implementation rules
P898: Ask each common symptom once. Cancer-specific pathways must reuse the recorded answer and ask only additional questions required for that pathway.
P899: Use conditional follow-ups. Show duration, progression, severity and associated-symptom questions only when the initial response is positive or uncertain and the follow-up is relevant.
P900: Keep uncertainty explicit. “Not sure,” “not asked” and “prefer not to answer” must not be silently converted to “No.”
P901: Route by evidence, not by symptom counting. A single symptom may be relevant to more than one pathway, but must not be counted multiple times to inflate a score.
P902: Keep safety separate from risk scoring. Urgent symptoms trigger the appropriate care guidance regardless of the general risk-factor profile or any calculated score.
P903: Do not generate an individual cancer probability from this common module. Display the symptom findings and appropriate next step. Cancer-specific risk estimates should only be shown when supported by a validated model applicable to the individual and population.
P904: Clinician review is mandatory before deployment. Final wording, escalation thresholds and timeframes must be approved for the intended setting.
P907: 1. Thyroid cancer pathway.
P908: TH001 — Previous thyroid abnormality
P909: Have you ever been told by a healthcare professional that you have a thyroid nodule, goitre or another thyroid condition?
P910: No
P911: Yes
P912: Not sure
P913: If yes, select all that apply:
P914: Thyroid nodule
P915: Goitre or enlarged thyroid
P916: Thyroiditis
P917: Hypothyroidism
P918: Hyperthyroidism
P919: Other thyroid condition
P920: Not sure
P921: Interpretation: a thyroid nodule or goitre is more directly relevant to evaluation of a thyroid lump than uncomplicated hypothyroidism or hyperthyroidism. Do not assign the same cancer-risk weight to all thyroid diagnoses.
P922: TH002 — Previous therapeutic radiation
P923: Have you received radiation treatment involving your head, neck or upper chest?
P924: No
P925: Yes
P926: Not sure
P927: If yes:
P928: Age at exposure: childhood / adolescence / adulthood / not sure
P929: Approximate age or year, if known: optional
P930: TH003 — Radioactive fallout
P931: Have you experienced a known substantial exposure to radioactive fallout or a major radiation incident?
P932: No known exposure
P933: Possible exposure
P934: Confirmed exposure
P935: Not sure
P936: Activation logic
P937: Activate the thyroid assessment pathway when any of the following is present:
P938: A new or enlarging lump in the thyroid/neck region, especially a documented thyroid nodule.
P939: An unexplained persistent voice change or swallowing/pressure symptom associated with a neck finding.
P940: A known thyroid nodule with a previous abnormal investigation or pending recommended follow-up.
P941: Previous childhood/adolescent head-and-neck radiation or substantial radioactive fallout should inform the risk profile and clinician review.
P942: They do not independently establish a cancer diagnosis.
P943: The relationship of radiation exposure, goitre and MEN2/RET to thyroid cancer is described by the NCI.
P944: Do not activate a high thyroid-cancer probability merely because a person has hypothyroidism or hyperthyroidism.
P947: 2. Oral cavity cancer pathway.
P948: Reuse common variables.
P949: C005: lump, if located in the mouth, lip, tongue, gums or jaw
P950: C006: unexplained oral bleeding
P951: C010: swallowing difficulty
P952: C015: mouth, jaw or facial pain
P953: Shared tobacco, alcohol and previous abnormal-lesion data
P954: Additional oral questions
P955: OR002 — Persistent oral lesion
P956: Have you had a sore or ulcer inside your mouth or on your lip that has not healed normally?
P957: No
P958: Yes
P959: Not sure
P960: OR003 — Persistent oral patch
P961: Have you noticed a persistent white, red or mixed red-and-white patch inside your mouth?
P962: No
P963: Yes
P964: Not sure
P965: OR004 — Oral growth or thickening
P966: Have you noticed a persistent lump, thickening or abnormal growth on your lip, tongue, gums, cheek or another part of your mouth?
P967: No
P968: Yes
P969: Not sure
P970: For any positive answer to OR002–OR004, ask:
P971: Location: lip / tongue / gum / inner cheek / floor of mouth / palate / other / not sure
P972: Duration: less than 2 weeks / 2–3 weeks / more than 3 weeks / not sure
P973: Increasing in size or worsening: yes / no / not sure
P974: OR005 — Other oral symptoms
P975: Have you experienced persistent unexplained numbness, pain or difficulty moving your tongue or jaw?
P976: No
P977: Yes
P978: Not sure
P979: If yes, ask the location and duration. Do not repeat the common swallowing question.
P980: Activation logic
P981: Activate the oral assessment pathway for:
P982: A persistent unexplained oral ulcer, patch, lump or thickening.
P983: An oral lesion that is growing, bleeding or associated with persistent pain or altered sensation.
P984: A concerning oral lesion in someone with areca/betel or tobacco exposure.
P985: A previous oral abnormality with incomplete recommended follow-up.
P986: Areca exposure contributes to the risk-factor profile even without symptoms; it is not itself proof of oral cancer.
P987: Persistent lesions warrant clinical examination.
P988: India's early-detection literature specifically recognises the importance of oral cancer assessment.
P992: 3. Head-and-neck cancer pathway
P993: This is an umbrella pathway for relevant head-and-neck symptoms.
P994: Oral cavity, laryngeal and hypopharyngeal subpathways should be opened when their own criteria are met.
P995: Reuse common variables
P996: C005: neck lump
P997: C009: coughing up blood
P998: C010: swallowing difficulty or pain
P999: C011: persistent voice change
P1000: C015: pain, with location
P1001: Shared tobacco, alcohol, areca, HPV and previous cancer data
P1002: Additional questions
P1003: HN001 — Persistent throat discomfort
P1004: Have you had a persistent sore throat or throat discomfort without an established explanation?
P1005: No
P1006: Yes
P1007: Not sure
P1008: If yes: duration under 2 weeks / 2–3 weeks / more than 3 weeks / not sure.
P1009: HN002 — One-sided ear pain
P1010: Have you experienced persistent unexplained pain in one ear, particularly alongside throat or swallowing symptoms?
P1011: No
P1012: Yes, right ear
P1013: Yes, left ear
P1014: Yes, either ear at different times
P1015: Not sure
P1016: HN003 — Difficulty opening the mouth or moving the tongue
P1017: Have you developed persistent difficulty opening your mouth or moving your tongue normally?
P1018: No
P1019: Yes
P1020: Not sure
P1021: If yes, record duration and whether it is worsening.
P1022: HN004 — HPV information
P1023: The shared HPV module should be consumed.
P1024: Do not ask again whether high-risk HPV was detected.
P1025: Activation logic
P1026: Activate the umbrella pathway for a persistent unexplained throat symptom, neck lump, one-sided ear pain with relevant throat symptoms, progressive swallowing difficulty, or restricted mouth/tongue movement.
P1027: Then route to the relevant anatomical subpathway:
P1028: Oral lesion or mouth/jaw abnormality → oral cavity
P1029: Persistent hoarseness → laryngeal
P1030: Throat-level swallowing symptoms with relevant findings → hypopharyngeal or other head-and-neck assessment
P1031: Thyroid-region lump → thyroid assessment
P1032: Tobacco, alcohol, areca and relevant HPV information should influence the clinical context, not independently prove that a symptomatic person has a head-and-neck cancer.
P1043: 4. Lung cancer pathway.
P1044: Reuse existing data
P1045: Tobacco/nicotine product, status, amount and duration
P1046: Second-hand smoke
P1047: Occupational and household smoke exposure
P1048: Previous cancer, family history and relevant medical history
P1049: C001: unintentional weight loss
P1050: C004: fatigue, if present
P1051: C009: coughing up blood
P1052: C011: voice change
P1053: C015: chest pain
P1054: C016: breathing difficulty
P1055: Additional questions
P1056: LU001 — Radon exposure.
P1057: Have you lived or worked for long periods in an enclosed building, underground mine or other location known to have elevated radon levels.
P1058: No known exposure
P1059: Possible exposure
P1060: Confirmed exposure
P1061: Not sure
P1062: If there is no reliable basis to assess radon exposure, do not force the user to guess.
P1063: LU002 — Persistent cough
P1064: Do you currently have a persistent or progressively worsening cough without an established explanation?
P1065: No
P1066: Yes
P1067: Not sure
P1068: If yes, duration: less than 2 weeks / 2–3 weeks / more than 3 weeks / not sure.
P1069: Activation logic
P1070: Activate the lung assessment pathway when there is:
P1071: Unexplained coughing up blood.
P1072: Persistent or progressively worsening cough.
P1073: Unexplained breathlessness or chest pain that warrants clinical assessment.
P1074: A relevant abnormal lung investigation needing follow-up.
P1075: A combination of persistent respiratory symptoms and concerning associated findings.
P1076: Tobacco exposure, occupational exposure and other established risk factors should be included in the separate lung risk profile. They do not replace symptom evaluation.
P1077: Do not treat a generic risk-factor total as lung-cancer probability. Any screening eligibility rules must be implemented separately and only when an evidence-based guideline applies to the population being assessed.
P1080: 5. Liver cancer pathway.
P1081: Reuse existing data
P1082: Chronic liver disease and cirrhosis
P1083: HBV/HCV
P1084: Fatty liver/MASLD
P1085: Alcohol exposure
P1086: Diabetes, obesity and relevant inherited disorders
P1087: Previous cancer
P1088: C001: weight loss
P1089: C012: abdominal swelling
P1090: C014: appetite loss or early fullness
P1091: C015: upper-right abdominal pain, if the location is recorded
P1092: Previous liver imaging or other investigations
P1093: Additional questions
P1094: LI001 — Jaundice
P1095: Have you developed unexplained yellowing of the skin or whites of your eyes?
P1096: No
P1097: Yes
P1098: Not sure
P1099: If yes, record onset and whether it is ongoing.
P1100: LI002 — Urine or stool colour change
P1101: Have you noticed unusually dark urine or persistently pale or clay-coloured stools without an established explanation?
P1102: No
P1103: Yes
P1104: Not sure
P1105: Activation logic
P1106: Activate liver assessment when jaundice or another concerning liver-related symptom is reported, a relevant abnormal investigation needs follow-up, or an existing liver condition requires clinician-directed cancer surveillance.
P1107: For the risk profile, give particular attention to established chronic liver disease, cirrhosis and HBV/HCV. Aflatoxin is a recognised carcinogenic exposure, but asking people whether food was contaminated is an unreliable way to quantify their individual exposure. I recommend removing the proposed aflatoxin question from the core pilot questionnaire rather than treating a subjective answer as a measurable risk score.
P1108: Do not assume that jaundice, abdominal swelling or dark urine means liver cancer; several liver, biliary and other conditions can cause these findings.
P1111: 6. Endometrial cancer pathway.
P1112: Only show anatomy- and history-relevant questions. Do not infer the presence of a uterus from gender identity.
P1113: Reuse existing data
P1114: Menopause status and age at menopause, if known
P1115: Menstrual and abnormal-bleeding history
P1116: BMI, using appropriate adult interpretation
P1117: Pregnancy history where relevant
P1118: Additional questions
P1119: EN001 — PCOS
P1120: Have you ever been diagnosed with polycystic ovary syndrome (PCOS)?
P1121: No
P1122: Yes
P1123: Not sure
P1124: EN002 — Endometrial hyperplasia
P1125: Have you ever been diagnosed with endometrial hyperplasia?
P1126: No
P1127: Yes
P1128: Not sure
P1129: If yes, ask whether recommended follow-up is complete:
P1130: Yes
P1131: No
P1132: Currently under care
P1133: Not sure
P1134: EN003 — Menopausal hormone therapy
P1135: Have you ever used menopausal hormone therapy?
P1136: No
P1137: Currently using it
P1138: Used it previously
P1139: Not sure
P1140: Not applicable
P1141: If yes:
P1142: Estrogen alone
P1143: Estrogen plus progestogen
P1144: Type not known
P1145: Not sure
P1146: Reuse rather than repeat
P1147: Use the shared reproductive module for postmenopausal bleeding and intermenstrual bleeding. Do not ask a second copy of these questions.
P1148: Activation logic
P1149: Activate the endometrial assessment pathway for unexplained postmenopausal bleeding, relevant abnormal uterine bleeding, or a previous endometrial abnormality needing follow-up.
P1150: PCOS, endometrial hyperplasia, relevant medication exposure, obesity, diabetes and hereditary risk belong in the risk-factor profile. They should not be combined into an unvalidated numerical cancer probability.
P1151: Unexplained postmenopausal bleeding warrants clinical assessment even if it occurred only once. Do not downgrade it because the patient has few other risk factors.
P1162: 7. Splenic and hematologic malignancy pathway.
P1163: This pathway should be named Splenic and Hematologic Assessment, not “spleen cancer risk.” An enlarged spleen or splenic lesion has multiple possible causes and does not automatically imply malignancy.
P1164: Additional questions
P1165: SH001 — Enlarged spleen
P1166: Has a healthcare professional ever told you that your spleen is enlarged?
P1167: No
P1168: Yes
P1169: Not sure
P1170: SH002 — Previous splenic abnormality
P1171: Has an imaging investigation shown a splenic mass, lesion or other unexplained splenic abnormality?
P1172: No
P1173: Yes
P1174: Not sure
P1175: If yes, ask whether a clinician is already following it and whether recommended follow-up is complete.
P1176: SH003 — Previous abnormal investigations
P1177: Have you been told that a blood count, blood smear, bone-marrow investigation or imaging study showed an unexplained abnormality involving the blood, bone marrow, lymph nodes or spleen?
P1178: No
P1179: Yes
P1180: Not sure
P1181: If yes, record the test type and whether follow-up is pending. Do not ask the patient to interpret technical results.
P1182: Reuse common variables
P1183: C001: weight loss
P1184: C002: fever
P1185: C003: drenching night sweats
P1186: C004: fatigue
P1187: C005: lumps, especially lymph-node-region lumps
P1188: C006: unexplained bruising or bleeding
P1189: Activation logic
P1190: Activate this pathway for a known unexplained splenic abnormality, clinically documented splenomegaly, relevant abnormal hematologic investigations, or a combination of concerning symptoms and findings.
P1191: A history of a previous hematologic malignancy should generally route to the person's existing treating or follow-up team rather than being scored as a new suspected cancer.
P1192: Do not activate a strong malignancy alert from fatigue, fever or night sweats in isolation.
P1195: 8. Colorectal cancer pathway.
P1196: Reuse common variables
P1197: C007: blood in stools or black stools
P1198: C013: persistent bowel-habit change
P1199: C001: unintentional weight loss
P1200: C015: abdominal pain, when relevant
P1201: Additional questions
P1202: CO001 — Incomplete bowel emptying
P1203: Do you frequently feel that your bowels have not emptied completely after passing stools?
P1204: No
P1205: Yes, occasionally
P1206: Yes, repeatedly
P1207: Yes, persistently or worsening
P1208: Not sure
P1209: CO002 — Bowel-related abdominal discomfort
P1210: Have you experienced persistent or recurrent abdominal cramps or discomfort associated with bowel movements?
P1211: No
P1212: Yes, occasionally
P1213: Yes, recurrently
P1214: Yes, persistently or worsening
P1215: Not sure
P1216: CO003 — Duration of bowel changes
P1217: If C013 is positive, reuse its duration answer. If duration was not collected, ask:
P1218: Less than 2 weeks
P1219: 2–4 weeks
P1220: More than 4 weeks
P1221: Not sure
P1222: Risk profile only
P1223: Reuse the diet module for processed meat and fibre-related dietary information. Do not repeat the processed-meat and fruit/vegetable questions inside the colorectal symptom pathway.
P1224: Diet is not a sufficient reason by itself to activate a symptomatic colorectal cancer pathway. Do not assign arbitrary symptom points to processed meat intake or low fruit and vegetable intake.
P1225: Activation logic
P1226: Activate colorectal assessment for unexplained blood in stools, persistent bowel-habit change, a relevant abnormal bowel investigation or other concerning combinations of findings. A positive symptom does not establish colorectal cancer, and the appropriate investigation is determined by a clinician.
P1229: 9. Cervical cancer pathway.
P1230: Only show this pathway when the person has a cervix or relevant cervical history. Do not assume anatomy from gender identity.
P1231: Reuse existing data
P1232: Cervical screening and HPV results
P1233: HPV vaccination history
P1234: Relevant family/medical history, if applicable
P1235: Existing abnormal cervical results and whether follow-up is complete
P1236: Shared bleeding and pain records where those variables have already been collected
P1237: Use Bleeding after sexual intercourse & vaginal discharge from previous questionnaire.
P1238: Remove the redundant questions
P1239: Do not ask a second copy of intermenstrual bleeding, postmenopausal bleeding or pelvic pain if these are already recorded in the common/reproductive modules.
P1240: Pain during intercourse, discharge colour, itching and burning should not be assigned independent cervical-cancer points. These findings can have many non-cancer causes.
P1241: Activation logic
P1242: Activate cervical assessment for unexplained postcoital bleeding, concerning persistent bleeding/discharge, or an abnormal cervical result with incomplete follow-up.
P1243: Separately, assess whether the person is due for guideline-based cervical screening. Screening eligibility is not the same as a symptomatic cancer pathway. WHO distinguishes screening of asymptomatic people from early diagnosis of people who already have symptoms.
P1246: 10. Stomach cancer pathway.
P1247: Reuse common variables
P1248: C001: weight loss
P1249: C010: swallowing difficulty, where relevant
P1250: C014: appetite loss or early fullness
P1251: C015: upper abdominal pain
P1252: Relevant family history, prior gastric abnormality and previous investigations
P1253: Shared diet module
P1254: Additional questions
P1255: ST001 — Persistent upper abdominal discomfort
P1256: Have you experienced unexplained pain or discomfort in the upper part of your abdomen?
P1257: No
P1258: Yes, occasionally
P1259: Yes, recurrently
P1260: Yes, persistently or worsening
P1261: Not sure
P1262: ST002 — Nausea or vomiting
P1263: Have you experienced persistent unexplained nausea or repeated vomiting?
P1264: No
P1265: Yes, nausea
P1266: Yes, repeated vomiting
P1267: Yes, both
P1268: Not sure
P1269: If vomiting blood or material resembling coffee grounds is reported, activate urgent medical assessment, not simply a stomach cancer score.
P1270: ST003 — Persistent indigestion
P1271: Have you experienced persistent or worsening indigestion without an established explanation?
P1272: No
P1273: Yes, occasional
P1274: Yes, recurrent
P1275: Yes, persistent or worsening
P1276: Not sure
P1277: Remove or reclassify
P1278: Do not repeat early satiety, swallowing difficulty, weight loss or appetite loss.
P1279: Do not activate a stomach cancer pathway from occasional reflux or indigestion alone.
P1280: Reuse the diet module for heavily salted or preserved food exposure; do not assign arbitrary cancer points to that response.
P1281: Activation logic
P1282: Activate stomach assessment when persistent upper abdominal symptoms, repeated unexplained vomiting, concerning associated findings or a previous abnormal gastric investigation warrant clinical review. Urgency is determined by symptom severity and the clinical picture, not by a sum of dietary risk factors.
P1285: 11. Oesophageal cancer pathway.

P1301: No

P1304: Yes, increasingly often
P1305: Not sure

P1307: Do not ask another swallowing-pain question if C010 has already captured it.
P1308: The question about extremely hot beverages may be retained as an optional descriptive exposure variable if the team has a defined evidence-based purpose, but it should not activate the oesophageal pathway by itself or contribute arbitrary points to an individual probability.
P1309: Activation logic
P1310: Activate oesophageal assessment for swallowing difficulty—especially if progressive—food sticking, relevant regurgitation or concerning associated findings. Inability to swallow saliva or liquids requires urgent assessment independently of the cancer-risk profile.

P1497: Breast Cancer Pathway.
P1498: BR001 — New breast or underarm change
P1499: Reuse C005. If the person reports a new lump or swelling, ask:
P1500: Location: right breast, left breast, both breasts, armpit, or another nearby area.
P1501: Has it increased in size? Yes / No / Not sure.
P1502: Is it new, longstanding, or previously assessed?
P1503: Has a clinician examined it? Yes / No / Appointment pending / Not sure.
P1504: Was imaging or a biopsy recommended, and is follow-up complete? Yes / No / Pending / Not sure.
P1505: Do not ask the person to judge whether a lump feels cancerous. That requires clinical assessment.
P1506: BR002 — Breast shape or size change
P1507: Question: Have you noticed a new or unexplained change in the size, shape, or contour of either breast?
P1508: No
P1509: Yes, right breast
P1510: Yes, left breast
P1511: Yes, both breasts
P1512: Not sure
P1513: If yes: Is the change increasing, and when did it begin?
P1514: BR003 — Breast skin change
P1515: Question: Have you noticed a new or unexplained change in the skin of either breast?
P1516: No
P1517: Dimpling, puckering, or pulling inward
P1518: Persistent redness or darkening
P1519: Thickened or unusually swollen skin
P1520: Peau d’orange appearance (skin resembling an orange peel)
P1521: Persistent rash, scaling, or a sore affecting the nipple or areola
P1522: Other change
P1523: Not sure
P1524: If positive, record the side, onset, progression, and whether it has been assessed previously.
P1525: BR004 — Nipple change
P1526: Question: Have you noticed a new change in either nipple?
P1527: No
P1528: A nipple has newly turned inward or changed direction
P1529: A persistent sore, crusting, or skin change on the nipple
P1530: Another unexplained change
P1531: Not sure

P1533: BR005 — Nipple discharge
P1534: Question: Have you noticed fluid coming from either nipple that is not expected breast milk?
P1535: No
P1536: Yes, one breast
P1537: Yes, both breasts
P1538: Not sure
P1539: If yes, ask:
P1540: Appearance: bloody, clear/watery, milky, yellow/green, or other/not sure.
P1541: Does it come out spontaneously or only when squeezed?
P1542: Is it repeated or persistent?
P1543: Is there also a lump or nipple/skin change?
P1544: Important: Do not automatically label all nipple discharge suspicious. Spontaneous, unilateral, bloody or clear discharge merits particular attention; discharge with other breast changes strengthens the need for assessment. Milky discharge can have non-cancer causes.
P1545: BR006 — Breast pain
P1546: Reuse C015 rather than adding a second pain question. If the pain is in the breast, record:
P1547: Side: right / left / both.
P1548: Location: one specific area / widespread / unsure.
P1549: Pattern: occasional / persistent / worsening.
P1550: Whether a lump, nipple change, or skin change is also present.
P1551: Breast pain alone is not a specific indicator of cancer, but persistent focal pain or pain with other changes warrants clinical review.
P1552: BR007 — Previous breast condition or investigation
P1553: Reuse the shared previous-abnormality and previous-cancer history. If applicable, capture:
P1554: Previous breast cancer or ductal carcinoma in situ (DCIS).
P1555: Previous breast biopsy or an abnormal breast imaging result.
P1556: A known high-risk breast lesion, if the patient knows its name.
P1557: A recommended repeat scan, biopsy, or specialist appointment that has not been completed.
P1560: Investigations.
P1561: Suspected cancer
P1562: Targeted initial investigations
P1563: Thyroid
P1564: TSH; thyroid/neck ultrasound for a relevant nodule or neck finding. Calcitonin only when medullary thyroid cancer is specifically suspected.
P1565: Oral cavity
P1566: Direct oral examination by a qualified clinician; biopsy of a suspicious lesion. No routine diagnostic blood tumour marker.
P1567: Head and neck
P1568: ENT examination; targeted endoscopy and imaging as clinically indicated; biopsy of a suspicious lesion or node.
P1569: Lung
P1570: Chest X-ray may be an initial investigation in appropriate symptomatic patients; diagnostic chest CT and tissue sampling if indicated. CBC is supportive, not diagnostic.
P1571: Liver
P1572: Liver function tests, bilirubin and appropriate HBV/HCV testing; liver ultrasound or other diagnostic imaging when indicated. AFP is an adjunct in selected settings.
P1573: Endometrial
P1574: Clinical assessment of abnormal bleeding; transvaginal ultrasound and/or endometrial sampling as indicated. CBC can assess blood loss.
P1575: Splenic/hematologic
P1576: CBC with differential and peripheral smear; further testing guided by abnormalities, potentially including flow cytometry, imaging or bone-marrow examination.
P1577: Colorectal
P1578: CBC and ferritin/iron studies if anaemia is suspected; FIT or colonoscopy according to symptoms and local guidance. A concerning presentation may require direct diagnostic assessment.
P1579: Cervical
P1580: Appropriate cervical examination, HPV testing/cytology in the relevant context, and colposcopy/biopsy for indicated abnormal results or suspicious findings.
P1581: Stomach
P1582: CBC and iron studies where indicated; upper GI endoscopy with biopsy when symptoms and clinical assessment warrant it.
P1583: Oesophageal
P1584: Upper GI endoscopy with biopsy for concerning dysphagia or other appropriate indications. Do not delay assessment for a blood-test score.
P1585: Leukaemia
P1586: CBC with differential and peripheral smear; urgent clinical review if concerning abnormalities are identified. Flow cytometry and bone-marrow studies are follow-up tests when indicated.
P1587: Ovarian
P1588: Pelvic/transvaginal ultrasound and CA-125 in appropriate clinical contexts. Neither a normal CA-125 nor a single symptom reliably excludes disease.
P1589: Non-Hodgkin lymphoma
P1590: CBC, LDH where indicated, and assessment of persistent unexplained lymphadenopathy; tissue biopsy is required for diagnosis.
P1591: Prostate
P1592: PSA after appropriate clinical discussion; urinalysis and other urinary assessment where indicated. PSA alone cannot diagnose prostate cancer.
P1593: Hypopharyngeal
P1594: ENT assessment, endoscopic examination and biopsy when indicated; imaging based on clinical findings.
P1595: Laryngeal
P1596: Laryngoscopy for persistent unexplained hoarseness when indicated; biopsy of a suspicious lesion.
P1597: Breast
P1598: Clinical breast examination and diagnostic ultrasound and/or mammography according to age and presentation; image-guided core biopsy if indicated. No routine serum tumour marker for initial diagnosis.
P1599: Assessment result
P1600: Initial investigation approach
P1601: Low score, no concerning symptoms
P1602: No cancer-specific blood panel automatically. Follow applicable screening guidance.
P1603: Intermediate score or relevant persistent symptoms
P1604: Targeted initial tests based on the symptom pattern and activated pathway.
P1605: High score or multiple concerning findings
P1606: Prioritised clinical assessment and pathway-specific investigations; urgent symptoms override the score.
P1607: Abnormal initial test or suspicious examination/imaging
P1608: Clinician-directed imaging, tissue sampling, histopathology, and selected IHC/molecular tests.
P1612: SCORING.
P1613: A. General risk-factor burden index — /100
P1614: Summarises recorded exposures and established background risk factors. In the pilot, it must be labelled experimental and non-predictive. It must not be described as the person's lifetime cancer risk.
P1615: B. Safety and symptom triage — rule-based
P1616: Determines whether a response requires emergency advice, urgent clinical assessment, prompt assessment or routine follow-up. No sum of points may override a safety rule.
P1617: C. Cancer-specific pathway assessment
P1618: Activates the relevant pathway using anatomy, symptoms, prior abnormalities, family history and other clinically relevant information. Its initial output should be a pathway status, not a fabricated cancer probability.
P1619: D. Validated cancer-specific risk model
P1620: Provides an absolute-risk estimate only when an appropriate model exists and is validated or adequately calibrated for the intended population, outcome and time horizon. Lifetime risk belongs here.
P1621: Why not simply add the general score to a cancer-specific score? Because the same tobacco history, weight loss or family-history item could then be counted several times. This creates an apparently precise number without a valid statistical interpretation.
P1622: 3. General score out of 100: how to design it properly
P1623: The first question is what the score is intended to measure.
P1624: For the ONCOGUIDE pilot, the defensible target is an experimental general risk-factor burden index, not “the chance of getting cancer.” It describes selected risk-factor information; it does not establish a patient's probability of developing cancer.
P1625: Candidate domains
P1626: How should the points be allocated?
P1627: Not by intuition and not by giving every question equal weight.
P1628: The statistically defensible process is:
P1629: Define the outcome and target population.
P1630: Identify evidence-supported predictors for that outcome.
P1631: Estimate the predictor coefficients using suitable data, or adopt an existing validated model where appropriate.
P1632: Evaluate the model on independent data and assess calibration and discrimination.
P1633: If a 0–100 display is desired, transform the model output to that scale using a documented method.
P1634: Validate the transformed score and the actions linked to its thresholds.
P1635: For example, a 100-point scale could be a rescaling of a validated model's predicted probability or linear predictor. But 100 points must not automatically mean 100% risk, and 50 points must not automatically mean 50% risk.
P1636: For the pilot, I would not yet assign arbitrary numerical weights to these domains and label the result “low, moderate or high cancer risk.” We do not have an established outcome model, coefficients or validated cutoffs that justify those labels.
P1637: Which details should not earn general cancer-risk points by default?
P1638: Ethnicity, simply because a person belongs to an ethnic group.
P1639: Gender identity or a person's choice to disclose it.
P1640: A symptom merely because it is present; symptoms belong primarily in triage and pathway activation.
P1641: A missing answer, “not sure” or “prefer not to say.”
P1642: Every abnormality in the same way, regardless of its clinical significance.
P1643: BMI without age-appropriate interpretation.
P1644: Ethnicity can be collected for demographic reporting and equity assessment, but its use as a predictor requires a defensible rationale and appropriate population evidence. It should not be used as a proxy for genetic risk.
P1645: How to show the result in the pilot
P1646: Instead of presenting an unvalidated score as a clinical probability, show:
P1647: Recorded risk factors and exposure history.
P1648: Important risk factors that are present.
P1649: Important information that is unknown.
P1650: Relevant cancer pathways activated.
P1651: Symptoms or previous abnormalities needing follow-up.
P1652: A clear statement that the index is experimental and does not estimate lifetime cancer probability.
P1653: This remains useful for testing the intake and routing system without claiming more than the evidence supports.
P1654: 4. Should every one of the 18 cancer pathways have a score out of 100?
P1655: Not yet as an individual cancer-risk score. The same 100-point design cannot validly predict thyroid, lung, breast, ovarian, colorectal and the other cancers, because their risk factors, outcomes, clinical presentations and available prediction models differ.
P1656: For the first version, use the following pathway-specific assessment framework. The pathway is activated by clinical rules; its status is determined by the finding and the action required, not by adding arbitrary points.
P1657: These are routing and assessment rules, not a validated score table. The final activation conditions and urgency thresholds need review against current site-specific guidelines for the intended Indian setting. The WHO early-diagnosis framework and IARC's India-specific recommendations support the separation of early diagnosis, screening and referral.
P1659: TDR
P1660: +2
P1661: What if the authorities specifically want a number out of 100 for every pathway?
P1662: There are two different options:
P1663: Option 1 — Pathway assessment completeness /100
P1664: Measures whether the relevant questions and required information have been collected. For example, 90/100 would mean the assessment is largely complete, not that cancer risk is 90/100. This can be useful for software testing and audit.
P1665: Option 2 — Cancer-specific risk index /100
P1666: Summarises evidence-supported predictors for a particular cancer. It would require a defined outcome, a justified model, an appropriate population, documented weights and independent validation. Until those conditions are met, the number must not be presented as an individual's cancer probability or used to make unvalidated clinical decisions.
P1667: 1. The scoring rules ONCOGUIDE should use
P1668: Before assigning points, we need a consistent calculation rule. Otherwise, the same patient could receive different scores depending on how the questions are answered.
P1669: Standard calculation protocol
P1670: No / absent = 0 points
P1671: Only when the question was actually answered and “No” is a valid response.
P1672: Yes = the specified item points
P1673: Points depend on the finding and its clinical significance—not merely on the number of positive answers.
P1674: Unknown / unanswered = missing
P1675: Do not silently treat missing answers as “No.” Display assessment completeness separately.
P1676: Safety override = independent of the score
P1677: An emergency symptom or a finding requiring prompt assessment must not be neutralised by a low total.
P1678: How to interpret a pathway score
P1679: For the initial prototype, I recommend these software-testing bands only, not clinically validated thresholds:
P1680: These bands are not yet referral cut-offs. They cannot establish or exclude cancer, and a score of 80 does not mean an 80% chance of cancer.
P1681: Clinical urgency is a separate output. A suspicious finding can require review even at a low score. Conversely, a high score caused by several nonspecific symptoms does not automatically justify CT, PET, biopsy or tumour-marker testing.
P1682: 2. General Risk-Factor Burden: 100 points
P1683: This is the common profile score calculated once for the patient. Its purpose is to summarise selected exposures and background factors—not to predict the probability of any particular cancer.
P1684: The following are proposed engineering weights for a first prototype. They are not published or validated coefficients.
P1685: How to allocate the points within each domain
P1686: For a transparent prototype, use evidence-graded item rules rather than arbitrary increments for every questionnaire answer.
P1687: 0 points: exposure or history absent.
P1688: Partial points: lower or limited exposure where a clinically defensible dose/duration category exists.
P1689: Full points: the highest predefined exposure category or a relevant high-risk history.
P1690: Not scored: unknown, unanswered, or an exposure for which the item has not been mapped to a defensible rule.
P1691: For example, tobacco points should reflect product, intensity, duration, current versus former use, and relevant site-specific evidence. Simply assigning 20 points to every person who has ever tried tobacco would be poorly designed.
P1692: The general score should not add age, ethnicity, gender identity, every symptom, and every medical condition indiscriminately. Age and anatomy may be essential in a specific pathway, but they need site-specific interpretation. A previous cancer also needs a distinct follow-up route rather than being treated as just another lifestyle point.
P1693: The NCI’s risk-model resources illustrate why absolute-risk estimation requires models designed for a specific cancer and population, not a universal sum of risk-factor points.
P1695: NCI
P1696: +2
P1697: 3. The 18 cancer pathways: numerical weights out of 100
P1698: For each pathway below, the listed values are maximum point allocations. They define the architecture for the first implementation. They are not effect sizes, odds ratios, or literature-derived clinical weights.
P1699: The allocation deliberately gives more weight to relevant symptoms and objective clinical findings than to background exposures. For pathways where an abnormal test or a known lesion is particularly important, that domain receives more points.
P1700: 1. Thyroid cancer pathway — 100 points
P1701: Why: a thyroid lump and its documented imaging/pathology are more directly relevant than nonspecific fatigue or weight change. Thyroid dysfunction alone should not be treated as evidence of thyroid cancer. A rapidly enlarging mass, airway symptoms or significant swallowing difficulty requires separate clinical urgency assessment.
P1702: 2. Oral cavity cancer pathway — 100 points
P1703: Why: a visible persistent lesion deserves more weight than an exposure alone. Tobacco and areca exposure are important, particularly in the Indian context, but absence of exposure must never suppress a concerning lesion. This pathway is consistent with the importance of oral-cancer early detection in India's evidence-based recommendations.
P1705: IARC
P1706: 3. Head-and-neck umbrella pathway — 100 points
P1707: This is an anatomical routing pathway, not an independent cancer type. It should help select the appropriate site-specific pathway rather than produce an additional cancer probability.
P1708: Why: this umbrella is useful for intake and navigation. To avoid double-counting, a single persistent hoarse voice may activate the laryngeal pathway and the umbrella route, but it must not be treated as two independent pieces of evidence in a combined patient-level score.
P1709: 4. Lung cancer pathway — 100 points
P1710: Why: both respiratory symptoms and objective imaging findings matter, while smoking and certain occupational exposures inform risk. Haemoptysis and acute/severe breathing difficulty need independent safety rules. Do not automatically recommend low-dose CT based on this experimental score; screening eligibility must follow a separately reviewed guideline.
P1711: 5. Liver cancer pathway — 100 points
P1712: Why: chronic liver disease and a documented lesion can be more informative than general symptoms. Jaundice is not cancer-specific, but it may require prompt evaluation. The pathway must not diagnose liver cancer from symptoms or recommend a tumour marker as a standalone test.
P1713: 6. Endometrial cancer pathway — 100 points
P1714: Only ask anatomy- and history-relevant questions, and do not infer reproductive anatomy from gender identity.
P1715: Why: abnormal bleeding is a key reason for assessment, and postmenopausal bleeding must not be dismissed because the total is low. The score cannot decide whether biopsy or imaging is needed; that depends on the clinical pathway and examination.
P1716: 7. Splenic / haematologic assessment pathway — 100 points
P1717: This is not a “spleen cancer” prediction score. Primary splenic malignancy is uncommon; the pathway is intended to route relevant findings for clinician assessment.
P1718: Why: documented organ enlargement, blood abnormalities and specialist findings should outweigh isolated nonspecific symptoms. Abnormal blood counts require interpretation in context and do not automatically indicate leukaemia or lymphoma.
P1719: 8. Colorectal cancer pathway — 100 points
P1720: Why: a positive screening test or documented lesion requires a defined follow-up route, independently of lifestyle burden. Major ongoing bleeding, faintness or signs of instability require urgent assessment rather than waiting for a calculated total.
P1721: 9. Cervical cancer pathway — 100 points
P1722: Why: screening results and their required follow-up are central. Vaccination lowers risk but does not replace appropriate screening or evaluation of symptoms. A positive HPV test is not equivalent to cancer; follow-up depends on the actual result, age and applicable guideline.
P1723: 10. Stomach cancer pathway — 100 points
P1724: Why: persistent indigestion alone is common and nonspecific; documented abnormalities and relevant history require greater attention. Vomiting blood, coffee-ground vomit or signs of major bleeding must trigger urgent assessment separately.
P1725: 11. Oesophageal cancer pathway — 100 points
P1726: Why: swallowing difficulty is more pathway-specific than general fatigue or appetite change. Inability to swallow saliva or liquids is a safety override and should not wait for the score.
P1727: 12. Leukaemia pathway — 100 points
P1728: Why: objective blood findings should receive substantial weight, but the score cannot interpret a CBC in isolation. Many benign conditions cause fatigue, fever or bruising. Serious bleeding, severe illness or a critical laboratory result requires appropriate urgent assessment.
P1729: 13. Ovarian cancer pathway — 100 points
P1730: Why: persistent symptom patterns and objective pelvic findings matter more than any single nonspecific symptom. Menstrual/reproductive questions should be asked only when relevant. Do not use CA-125 or ultrasound as an automatic consequence of an arbitrary score; the decision belongs in a clinician-reviewed pathway.
P1731: 14. Non-Hodgkin lymphoma pathway — 100 points
P1732: Why: lymph-node characteristics, persistence and documented investigation findings matter. A short-lived tender node during an infection should not be scored equivalently to a persistent unexplained node. Itching alone is nonspecific and should not carry a large independent weight.
P1733: 15. Prostate cancer pathway — 100 points
P1734: Only activate this pathway where a prostate is present and the questions are clinically relevant.
P1735: Why: urinary symptoms are frequently caused by benign conditions and should not receive excessive weight by themselves. A PSA result is not a cancer diagnosis, and an experimental score should not automatically order PSA testing or biopsy.
P1736: 16. Hypopharyngeal cancer pathway — 100 points
P1737: This is another specific anatomical route within head-and-neck assessment.
P1738: Why: persistent focal symptoms and direct investigation findings should outweigh general lifestyle factors. Avoid counting a single swallowing symptom repeatedly across the hypopharyngeal, oesophageal and umbrella head-and-neck totals when producing an overall patient summary.
P1739: 17. Laryngeal cancer pathway — 100 points
P1740: Why: persistent voice change is a key pathway trigger, but short-lived hoarseness commonly has benign causes. Noisy breathing at rest or severe breathing difficulty must trigger urgent assessment regardless of the score.
P1741: 18. Breast cancer pathway — 100 points
P1742: Why: a new lump or suspicious imaging finding must not be diluted by a low background risk profile. Anatomy and reproductive history should be collected appropriately, and the model must not automatically recommend serum tumour markers for initial breast-cancer diagnosis.
P1743: 4. How to convert each domain into actual points
P1744: The tables above define the point budget for each pathway. The next rule determines how those points are assigned to individual answers, so the software can calculate a reproducible result.
P1745: A. Objective finding domain
P1746: Use the maximum allocation for the domain as a ceiling. Within it, assign points according to the strongest documented finding:
P1747: These are proposed software rules, not validated clinical likelihood ratios. The exact point allocation must be adjusted for each pathway: a positive HPV result, a suspicious breast image, and an abnormal CBC are not interchangeable findings.
P1748: B. Symptom domain
P1749: Do not award full points merely because a symptom exists. Use the clinical characteristics captured by the questionnaire.
P1750: The system must not assume that every symptom is unexplained simply because the patient answered “Yes.” If duration, progression or explanation is unknown, mark the relevant component incomplete.
P1751: C. Risk-factor domain
P1752: Risk factors should be scored by their relevant exposure categories, not simply by the number of positive answers.
P1753: For example, the tobacco rule should account for product type, duration, intensity and current/former use. A known hereditary pathogenic variant should be handled differently from an unverified report that a distant relative had cancer.
P1754: Important: these percentages are an initial point-allocation convention, not a claim that risk rises by those percentages. Before real clinical use, a clinician and methodologist should review the item-level mappings and the evidence supporting them.
P1755: 5. Preventing double-counting
P1756: Your ONCOGUIDE questionnaire contains a common symptom section and multiple cancer-specific pathways. That is sensible for intake, but it creates a statistical problem if one symptom earns points repeatedly.
P1757: I recommend these rules:
P1758: Ask common symptoms once, then reuse the answer in relevant pathways.
P1759: Within a pathway, use the highest applicable severity level for overlapping descriptions of the same symptom; do not award points twice for “persistent cough” and “cough lasting several weeks” if they describe the same episode.
P1760: Count distinct risk factors separately only where the scoring specification explicitly permits it.
P1761: Do not add the 18 pathway scores together to create a patient-level “total cancer risk.”
P1762: A pathway activation is not a diagnosis. A patient may activate more than one pathway.
P1763: Missing answers must not be silently converted into negative answers.
P1764: For a score to be meaningful, you also need to decide whether you are developing a risk prediction model, an assessment-priority index, or a referral decision rule. These are different products and require different evidence.
P1765: 6. How ONCOGUIDE should display the result
P1766: For every pathway, the interface should display the score and its limitations together.
P1767: Example: Breast pathway
P1768: Experimental assessment-priority index
P1769: 60 / 100
P1770: Prototype only
P1771: Contributing findings
P1772: New breast lump reported.
P1773: Relevant clinical examination not yet recorded.
P1774: Family history incomplete.
P1775: Interpretation
P1776: The recorded findings warrant clinical review according to the breast pathway. The score is not the probability that cancer is present, and a lower score would not rule cancer out.
P1777: Illustrative display only; the example score is not generated from a validated model.
P1778: In production, the interface should also show:
P1779: The individual items contributing points.
P1780: Which answers were missing or uncertain.
P1781: Why a pathway was activated.
P1782: Whether a safety override was triggered.
P1783: The evidence source and version of the rule set.
P1784: The next action, approved by the clinical governance team.
P1785: 7. What makes these numbers defensible before an authority?
P1786: The honest answer is that the architecture is defensible as a transparent prototype; the numerical weights are not yet defensible as clinically validated thresholds.
P1787: To move from a prototype to a clinical prediction model, ONCOGUIDE needs a defined target outcome, appropriate development data, internal validation, independent external validation, calibration assessment and evidence that using the model improves decisions without unacceptable harm. The TRIPOD reporting guidance and NCI's model-development resources describe these requirements.
P1789: PMC
P1790: +2
P1791: For India, screening and early-detection pathways should also be aligned with applicable site-specific guidance. IARC's recommendations for India specifically discuss breast, cervical and oral cancers; those recommendations should not be extrapolated into a universal score for all 18 pathways.
P1793: IARC
P1794: My recommendation
P1795: Use the numbers above as ONCOGUIDE's version 0.1 scoring specification for synthetic testing and clinician review. Keep the general risk-factor burden score separate from each cancer pathway, and keep urgent clinical findings outside both scores.
P1796: Before I convert this into script.js, the most important next step is to establish the exact question IDs, answer choices, persistence definitions and existing code for your questionnaire. Then the scoring can be implemented consistently, with test cases to verify that a concerning symptom or abnormal result is never hidden by a low numerical total.

P1932: “होय” हा पर्याय निवडल्यास पुढील माहिती विचारावी:
P1933: एकूण किती महिने स्तनपान केले आहे?
P1934: महिन्यांची संख्या भरण्याची सुविधा असावी. संख्या वाढवण्यासाठी वरचे आणि कमी करण्यासाठी खालचे बाण असावेत.
P1935: सध्या स्तनपान सुरू आहे.
P1936: माहिती उपलब्ध नाही.
P1937: सध्या स्तनपान सुरू असल्यास:
P1938: स्तनपानाचा कालावधी विचारावा.
P1939: कालावधी भरण्यासाठी संख्या वाढवण्याचे आणि कमी करण्याचे बाण असावेत.
P1940: क) तुम्हाला योनीतून किंवा गर्भाशयाच्या मुखातून असामान्य स्त्राव जाणवला आहे का?
P1941: नाही
P1942: होय
P1943: सांगता येत नाही
P1944: “होय” हा पर्याय निवडल्यासच पुढील प्रश्न विचारावेत.
P1945: ड) स्त्रावाचा रंग कोणता आहे?
P1946: एकापेक्षा अधिक पर्याय निवडता आले पाहिजेत.
P1947: पारदर्शक
P1948: पांढरा किंवा दुधासारखा
P1949: क्रीमसारखा किंवा फिकट पांढरा
P1950: पिवळा
P1951: गडद पिवळा
P1952: हिरवा
P1953: राखाडी किंवा राखाडी-पांढरा
P1954: गुलाबी
P1955: तपकिरी किंवा गंजट रंगाचा
P1956: लाल किंवा रक्तमिश्रित
P1957: इतर
P1958: सांगता येत नाही
P1959: “इतर” हा पर्याय निवडल्यास रंगाचे वर्णन लिहिण्याची जागा द्यावी.
P1960: ई) हा असामान्य स्त्राव किती काळापासून होत आहे?
P1961: एका आठवड्यापेक्षा कमी
P1962: १–४ आठवडे

P2005: १. आरोग्यसेवा व्यावसायिकाने तुम्हाला एचपीव्ही संसर्ग किंवा गर्भाशयाच्या मुखाच्या तपासणीतील असामान्य निष्कर्षाबद्दल कधी सांगितले आहे का?
P2006: नाही
P2007: होय, एचपीव्ही आढळला होता.
P2008: होय, गर्भाशयाच्या मुखाच्या तपासणीत असामान्य निष्कर्ष किंवा पूर्वकर्करोगजन्य बदल आढळले होते.
P2009: होय, दोन्ही आढळले होते.
P2010: खात्री नाही.
P2011: माझी गर्भाशयाच्या मुखाची तपासणी कधीही झालेली नाही.
P2012: २. या निष्कर्षासाठी तुम्हाला वैद्यकीय सल्ला किंवा पुढील तपासणी मिळाली आहे का?
P2013: होय, सुचवलेली पुढील तपासणी पूर्ण केली आहे.
P2014: होय, सध्या वैद्यकीय देखरेखीखाली आहे.
P2015: नाही, अद्याप पुढील तपासणी केलेली नाही.
P2016: कोणती पुढील तपासणी आवश्यक आहे, याची मला खात्री नाही.
P2017: ओन्कोगाइडने केवळ जोखीम गुणांवर अवलंबून न राहता योग्य वैद्यकीय तपासणी आणि पुढील उपचारांसाठी मार्गदर्शन करावे.

P2121: अ) तुम्ही कधी तंबाखू किंवा निकोटीन असलेले कोणतेही उत्पादन वापरले आहे का?

P2165: ग) सामान्यतः एका दिवसात तुम्ही त्या उत्पादनाचे किती प्रमाण वापरत होता/होता?
P2166: संख्यात्मक माहिती भरण्याची सुविधा असावी. संख्या वाढवण्यासाठी वरचे आणि कमी करण्यासाठी खालचे बाण असावेत.
P2167: सांगता येत नाही.
P2168: मापनाचे एकक निवडलेल्या उत्पादनानुसार आपोआप बदलले पाहिजे. प्रत्येक उत्पादनाचे प्रमाण सिगारेटच्या समतुल्य प्रमाणात रूपांतरित करण्यास वापरकर्त्याला सांगू नये.
P2169: सिगारेट किंवा बिडी: दररोज किती नग.
P2170: तोंडात ठेवून वापरण्याची तंबाखू: दररोज किती पाकिटे, पुड्या किंवा वेळा.
P2171: हुक्का: आठवड्यातून किती सत्रे.
P2172: ह) इतर लोकांच्या तंबाखूच्या धुराचा संपर्क तुम्हाला आला आहे का?
P2173: असा संपर्क झाल्याचे माहीत नाही.

P2175: नियमित संपर्क येतो.
P2176: दररोज संपर्क येतो.

P2178: इ) इतरांच्या तंबाखूच्या धुराचा नियमित संपर्क कोणत्या वयात सुरू झाला?
P2179: वय अंकांमध्ये भरण्याची सुविधा असावी. वय वाढवण्यासाठी वरचे आणि कमी करण्यासाठी खालचे बाण असावेत.

P2216: ३. पान, सुपारी आणि अरेका नटचा वापर

P2245: व्यावसायिक संपर्क आणि घरातील वायुप्रदूषण
P2246: अ) तुम्ही अशा कामात कधी काम केले आहे का, ज्यामध्ये हानिकारक धूळ, धूर, रसायने किंवा किरणोत्सर्गाचा संपर्क येण्याची शक्यता होती?
P2247: नाही
P2248: होय, सध्याच्या कामात.
P2249: होय, पूर्वीच्या कामात.
P2250: खात्री नाही.
P2251: ब) कामाच्या ठिकाणी तुम्हाला खालीलपैकी कोणत्या गोष्टींचा संपर्क आला होता?
P2252: लागू असलेले सर्व पर्याय निवडा. नियमित संपर्क तसेच अनेक वर्षे झालेला संपर्कही समाविष्ट करा.
P2253: ॲस्बेस्टॉस असलेल्या छताच्या पत्र्या, उष्णतारोधक साहित्य किंवा इतर बांधकाम साहित्य
P2254: दगड कापताना, खाणकामात, वाळूच्या जोरदार फवाऱ्याने स्वच्छता करताना किंवा बांधकामात निर्माण होणारी धूळ
P2255: खाणीतून किंवा कोळसा हाताळताना निर्माण होणारी कोळशाची धूळ
P2256: डिझेल इंजिन, ट्रक, बस किंवा जनरेटरमधून निघणारा धूर
P2257: वेल्डिंग करताना निर्माण होणारा धूर किंवा वायू
P2258: धातू कापताना, घासताना, वितळवताना किंवा धातूवर प्रक्रिया करताना निर्माण होणारी धूळ किंवा वायू
P2259: सुतारकाम, करवत गिरणी किंवा फर्निचर बनवताना निर्माण होणारी लाकडाची धूळ
P2260: तीव्र औद्योगिक स्वच्छताद्रव्ये, तेलकटपणा काढणारी रसायने, पातळ करणारी द्रव्ये किंवा विद्रावक
P2261: पेट्रोलियम शुद्धीकरण कारखाने, रासायनिक कारखाने किंवा इंधन प्रक्रियेत वापरली जाणारी रसायने
P2262: रंग, रंगाची फवारणी, वार्निश किंवा रंग पातळ करणारी द्रव्ये
P2263: रबर, प्लास्टिक किंवा टायर बनवताना वापरली जाणारी रसायने
P2264: शेतीतील कीटकनाशके, कीटक मारणारी औषधे किंवा पिकांवर फवारली जाणारी रसायने
P2265: प्रयोगशाळा, कारखाने किंवा पदार्थ जतन करण्याच्या कामात वापरली जाणारी फॉर्मल्डिहाइडयुक्त रसायने
P2266: बेंझीन असलेली रसायने किंवा उत्पादने, माहिती असल्यास
P2267: वैद्यकीय, औद्योगिक किंवा संशोधन उपकरणांमधून येणारा किरणोत्सर्ग
P2268: कामाच्या स्वरूपामुळे दीर्घकाळ उन्हात राहणे
P2269: इतर कामाच्या ठिकाणच्या प्रक्रियांमधून निर्माण होणारी भरपूर धूळ किंवा धूर
P2270: कामाच्या ठिकाणचा इतर प्रकारचा संपर्क
P2271: कोणत्या पदार्थाचा संपर्क आला होता हे माहीत नाही
P2272: या सर्व संपर्कांना समान कर्करोग-जोखीम देऊ नये. प्रत्येक संपर्काचा संबंध उपलब्ध वैज्ञानिक पुराव्यांनुसार स्वतंत्रपणे तपासावा.

P2373: HRL001. कर्करोगात रूपांतर होण्याची शक्यता असल्यामुळे एखाद्या असामान्य वाढीवर किंवा शरीरातील बदलावर लक्ष ठेवण्याची किंवा उपचार करण्याची गरज असल्याचे डॉक्टर, दंतवैद्य किंवा इतर आरोग्यसेवा व्यावसायिकांनी तुम्हाला कधी सांगितले आहे का?

P2377: “होय” निवडल्यास पुढील प्रश्न विचारा.
P2378: HRL002. तुम्हाला कोणत्या प्रकारच्या बदलाबद्दल सांगण्यात आले होते?
P2379: लागू असलेले सर्व पर्याय निवडा.
P2380: तोंडाच्या आतील भागात पांढरा किंवा लाल चट्टा
P2381: तंबाखू, गुटखा किंवा तत्सम पदार्थांशी संबंधित तोंड उघडताना येणारी घट्टपणा किंवा अडचण
P2382: गर्भाशयाच्या आतील आवरणातील असामान्य बदल
P2383: दीर्घकाळचा यकृतविकार किंवा यकृतावर व्रण पडणे
P2384: गर्भाशयाच्या मुखाच्या तपासणीतील असामान्य निष्कर्ष
P2385: आतड्यातील असामान्य वाढ किंवा पॉलिप
P2386: इतर असामान्य वाढ किंवा बदल
P2387: त्या स्थितीचे नाव आठवत नाही
P2388: HRL003. या स्थितीबद्दल सांगितल्यानंतर काय झाले?
P2389: शिफारस केलेले उपचार किंवा पुढील तपासणी पूर्ण केली.
P2390: सध्या उपचार किंवा पुढील तपासणी सुरू आहे.
P2391: पुन्हा तपासणीसाठी येण्यास सांगितले होते, पण अद्याप गेलो/गेले नाही.
P2392: यानंतर मला कोणताही पुढील सल्ला मिळाला नाही.
P2393: आता पुढे काय करावे हे मला माहीत नाही.
P2394: ओन्कोगाइड — सामान्य लक्षणे आणि धोक्याची सूचना देणारी लक्षणे

P2397: C001 — अनावधानाने वजन कमी होणे
P2398: प्रश्न: आहार किंवा शारीरिक हालचालींमध्ये बदल न करता तुमचे वजन कमी झाले आहे का?

४. वजन अजूनही कमी होत आहे का? होय / नाही / खात्री नाही
P2406: पुढील मार्गदर्शन: इतर लक्षणे, तपासणीतील निष्कर्ष आणि जोखीम घटक यांच्या आधारे संबंधित तपासणीचा मार्ग निवडावा. केवळ वजन कमी झाल्याने विशिष्ट कर्करोगाचे निदान होत नाही.

P2416: पुढील मार्गदर्शन: इतर निष्कर्षांसह दीर्घकाळ टिकणारा किंवा वारंवार येणारा अस्पष्ट ताप रक्ताशी संबंधित किंवा इतर योग्य तपासणीची गरज दर्शवू शकतो. तीव्र आजाराचे स्वतंत्रपणे मूल्यमापन करावे.

३. रक्तस्राव खूप आहे, सुरूच आहे किंवा थांबवणे कठीण आहे का? होय / नाही / लागू नाही / खात्री नाही
P2455: पुढील मार्गदर्शन: योग्य असल्यास रक्ताशी संबंधित आजारांचे मूल्यमापन करावे; मात्र रक्तस्रावाचे प्रत्यक्ष ठिकाण आणि इतर कारणेही तपासावीत. जास्त किंवा न थांबणारा रक्तस्राव असल्यास कर्करोगाच्या जोखीमेपासून स्वतंत्रपणे तातडीची वैद्यकीय तपासणी आवश्यक आहे.
P2456: C007 — शौचात रक्त किंवा काळे शौच
P2457: प्रश्न: शौचामध्ये रक्त दिसले आहे का किंवा शौच काळे, डांबरासारखे दिसले आहे का?

P2464: खात्री नाही
P2465: “होय” असल्यास:
१. हे एकदाच झाले की वारंवार? एकदा / वारंवार / खात्री नाही
२. रक्त शौचामध्ये मिसळलेले होते की मुख्यतः शौचाच्या पृष्ठभागावर किंवा टॉयलेट पेपरवर होते? शौचामध्ये मिसळलेले / पृष्ठभागावर किंवा कागदावर / खात्री नाही / लागू नाही
P2466: पुढील मार्गदर्शन: मोठे आतडे, गुदाशय आणि इतर पचनसंस्थेच्या समस्यांसाठी योग्य तपासणीचा विचार करावा. मोठ्या आतड्याच्या प्रश्नावलीत शौचातील रक्ताचा तोच प्रश्न पुन्हा विचारू नये.
P2467: सुरक्षेची सूचना: काळे, डांबरासारखे शौच किंवा मोठ्या प्रमाणातील रक्तस्राव हा पचनसंस्थेतील रक्तस्रावाचा संकेत असू शकतो आणि तातडीच्या तपासणीची गरज असू शकते. त्याचे कारण कर्करोगच आहे असे गृहीत धरू नये.
